- **clean-room-reference-implementation**: Analyze permitted reference behavior and independently build/test interoperable GIS functionality using provenance and evidence gates. ([ahmadibrahim4geo/GIS-Development-Skills](https://github.com/ahmadibrahim4geo/GIS-Development-Skills/tree/HEAD/skills/clean-room-reference-implementation) · ★6)
- **clean-room-reproducibility**: Use when auditing or hardening scion-defaults (or any GCP/GKE bootstrap CLI and template compiler) so external operators can reproduce deployments on a clean machine and fresh Google Cloud organization without hidden author state. ([duboc/scion-defaults](https://github.com/duboc/scion-defaults/tree/HEAD/skills/clean-room-reproducibility))
- **clean-room-reproduction**: Reproduce a finding from a fresh state with no residue from your own testing, so the observation is provably caused by the defect rather than by your setup. Use when a finding must be confirmed before reporting or after your environment has been heavily mutated ([Wsh7Ash/agentwall](https://github.com/Wsh7Ash/agentwall/tree/HEAD/skills/clean-room-reproduction) · 2 copies)
- **clean-room-research**: Use before reviewing any external policy engine, government-adjacent repository, academic implementation, or third-party model for architectural or capability research — e.g. OpenFisca Aotearoa, PolicyEngine NZ, BetterRules, or any other prior-art system. Governs how that inspection is allowed to happen so it never becomes the source of a Poltika policy value, formula, or rule. ([hemmingsenpeter-byte/poltika](https://github.com/hemmingsenpeter-byte/poltika/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/clean-room-research))
